### Q41. JEE Main 2023 (08 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>In photoelectric effect
A. The photocurrent is proportional to the intensity of the incident radiation.
B. Maximum kinetic energy with which photoelectrons are emitted depends on the intensity of incident light.
C. Max K.E. with which photoelectrons are emitted depends on the frequency of incident light.
D. The emission of photoelectrons require a minimum threshold intensity of incident radiation.
E. Max K.E. of the photoelectrons is independent of the frequency of the incident light.
Choose the correct answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> only
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></math> only
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> only
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> only**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>With proper energetic radiation, the photoelectric current increases with an increase in accelerating potential until it reaches a point where it reaches its maximum and does not increase with an additional increase in accelerating potential.</p><p>For a given potential difference, the photocurrent is directly proportional to the intensity of the incident radiation.</p><p>The maximum kinetic energy of the emitted photoelectrons can be written as</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mi>&#957;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#966;</mi></math></p><p>From above equation, it can be seen that the maximum kinetic energy of the photoelectron depends on the frequency of the light used.</p><p>Hence, the statements A and C are correct.</p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q42. JEE Main 2023 (06 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>The work functions of Aluminium and Gold are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math> respectively. The ratio of the slope of the stopping potential versus frequency plot for Gold to that of Aluminium is</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>24</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The data given is </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#981;</mi><mrow><mi>A</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#981;</mi><mrow><mi>A</mi><mi>u</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math></p><p>The energy is given by </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi><mi>&#957;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>h</mi><mi>&#957;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>e</mi><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p><p>where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> is the stopping potential and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>e</mi><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p><p>Thus, the above equation can be written as</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>e</mi><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mi>&#957;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>&#957;</mi></mrow><mi>e</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>&#981;</mi><mi>e</mi></mfrac></math></p><p>The equation is of the form of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi></math>. The slope is </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>h</mi><mi>e</mi></mfrac></math>.</p><p>Since the Planck constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi></math> and charge of an electron is a constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>e</mi></math>, the slope does not depend on the work function. Hence the ratio of the slope of the plot of stopping potential and frequency for both the given metals is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>.</p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q43. JEE Main 2023 (01 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The threshold frequency of metal is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>. When the light of frequency <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> is incident on the metal plate, the maximum velocity of photoelectron is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>. When the frequency of incident radiation is increased to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>. the maximum velocity of photoelectrons emitted is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>. The ratio of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>16</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Maximum kinetic energy is related to the incident frequency <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi></math> and the threshold frequency <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> as:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mi>f</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>h</mi><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p><p>When, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>h</mi><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>h</mi><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>When, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>h</mi><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>h</mi><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>h</mi><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>Hence, the ratio, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q44. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the two metals <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> are exposed to radiation of wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>350</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math>. The work functions of metals <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>and</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math>. Then choose the correct option

**Options:**

- **(A)** Metal <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> will not emit photo-electrons
- **(B)** Both metals <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> will emit photo-electrons
- **(C)** Both metals <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> will not emit photoelectrons
- **(D)** Metal <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> will not emit photo-electrons

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Metal <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> will not emit photo-electrons**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>As we know, for photoelectric emission, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi><mi>&#957;</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math>.</p><p>Now,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi><mi>&#957;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1240</mn><mn>350</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>54</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math></p><p>Therefore, only metal <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> will emit photoelectrons.</p>

</details>

---

### Q45. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>If a source of electromagnetic radiation having power <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>15</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kW</mi></math> produces <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>16</mn></msup></math> photons per second, the radiation belongs to a part of spectrum is :</p><p>(Take Planck constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Micro waves
- **(B)** Ultraviolet rays
- **(C)** Gamma rays
- **(D)** Radio waves

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Gamma rays**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>As energy of one photon is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>Power</mi><mrow><mi>Photon</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>frequency</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>Or, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mi>&#957;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>15</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>16</mn></msup></mfrac></math></p><p>So, frequency of ray is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#957;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>15</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>13</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>21</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Hz</mi></math>.</p><p>Thus, the radiation belongs to gamma rays.</p>

</details>

---

### Q46. JEE Main 2023 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A point source of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></math> emits light with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>%</mo></math> efficiency. At a distance of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> from the source, the intensity produced by the electric field component is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>40</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>20</mn></mfrac><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>40</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Total power emitted <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>100</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></math></p><p>Now intensity due to electric field will be half of the total intensity. Therefore,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>I</mi><mi>E</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mtext>power</mtext><mtext>area</mtext></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>40</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q47. JEE Main 2023 (30 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

A point source of light is placed at the centre of curvature of a hemispherical surface. The source emits a power of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>24</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></math>. The radius of curvature of hemisphere is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math> and the inner surface is completely reflecting. The force on the hemisphere due to the light falling on it is ______ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f77b1650-ffae-4a1f-a758-ae0c0b177a27-9895767.png" style="width: 300px; height: 243px;" /></p><p>For completely reflecting surface, force, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>I</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow><mi>c</mi></mfrac></math>, where, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>P</mi><mrow><mi>Surface</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>area</mi></mrow></mfrac></math> is intensity of radiation, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> is surface area and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi></math> is speed of light.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>I</mi></mrow><mi>c</mi></mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>c</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>24</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q48. JEE Main 2023 (30 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A small object at rest, absorbs a light pulse of power <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mW</mi></math> and duration <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ns</mi></math>. Assuming speed of light as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The momentum of the object becomes equal to :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>17</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>17</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>17</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>17</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>17</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Momentum <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>Energy</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>Speed</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>light</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>Power</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>time</mi></mrow><mi>c</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfenced><mrow><mn>20</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mn>300</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>17</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>As momentum is conserved, therefore momentum of object will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>17</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q49. JEE Main 2023 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>The threshold wavelength for photoelectric emission from a material is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#8728;</mo></mover></math>. Photoelectrons will be emitted, when this material is illuminated with monochromatic radiation from a</p><p>A. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>75</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></math> infra-red lamp</p><p>B. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></math> infra-red lamp</p><p>C. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>75</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></math> ultra-violet lamp</p><p>D. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></math> ultra-violet lamp</p><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below :</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** B and C only
- **(B)** A and D only
- **(C)** C only
- **(D)** C and D only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) C and D only**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given here, threshold wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mi>th</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>5500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#197;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>550</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math></p><p>For photoelectric emission, the wavelength of the light should be less than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>550</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math>.</p><p>Wavelength of infrared radiation extends from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>700</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mm</mi></math> and that of ultraviolet radiation is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>400</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math>.</p><p>Since, UV light has less wavelength than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>550</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math>, so only UV would be able to emit photoelectrons.</p>

</details>

### Q50. JEE Main 2023 (25 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>Given below are two statements :</p><p><strong>Statement I :</strong> Stopping potential in photoelectric effect does not depend on the power of the light source.</p><p><strong>Statement II :</strong> For a given metal, the maximum kinetic energy of the photoelectron depends on the wavelength of the incident light.</p><p>In the light of above statements, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below.</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement I is incorrect but statement II is correct
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect
- **(C)** Statement I is correct but statement II is incorrect
- **(D)** Both statement I and statement II are correct

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Both statement I and statement II are correct**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Stopping potential depends on frequency of incident light and nature of the emitter material. It is independent of the power of the light source.</p><p>Maximum kinetic energy of photoelectrons is stated as<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mi>&#957;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math>, Where, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi></math> is the Planck&rsquo;s constant, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#957;</mi></math> is the frequency, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#955;</mi></math> is wavelength of incident light and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#981;</mi></math> is the work function of the metal surface.</p><p>Thus, the maximum kinetic energy of the photoelectron depends on the wavelength of the incident light.</p><p>So both statements I and II are correct</p>

</details>

### Q51. JEE Main 2023 (24 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

From the photoelectric effect experiment, following observations are made. Identify which of these are correct
A. The stopping potential depends only on the work function of the metal.
B. The saturation current increases as the intensity of incident light increases.
C. The maximum kinetic energy of a photo electron depends on the intensity of the incident light.
D. Photoelectric effect can be explained using wave theory of light.
Choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** B, C only
- **(B)** A, C, D only
- **(C)** B only
- **(D)** A, B, D only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) B only**

**Detailed Solution:**

(A) Stopping potential depends on both frequency of light and work function.
(B) Saturation current <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8733;</mo></math> intensity of light.
(C) Maximum KE depends on frequency.
(D) Photoelectric effect is explained using particle theory. In this phenomenon, when electromagnetic radiation (such as light) hits the material, the emission of electrons takes place. In the photoelectric effect, if the frequency is too low, no electron is seen getting freed. But, if the frequency is high enough, some electrons can be observed. These observations prove that the Light is made of particles. The energy of the particle increases with the frequency.

</details>

### Q52. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Two streams of photons, possessing energies to five and ten times the work function of metal are incident on the metal surface successively. The ratio of the maximum velocities of the photoelectron emitted, in the two cases respectively, will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>3</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>3</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>2</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>3</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Using Einstein&#39;s photoelectric equation,</p><p>Maximum kinetic energy, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math>, where, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi></math> is energy of photon and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#981;</mi></math> is work function.</p><p>For two cases, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#981;</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p>Thus, the ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>9</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q53. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>With reference to the observations in photo-electric effect, identify the correct statements from below:</p><p>A. The square of maximum velocity of photoelectrons varies linearly with frequency of incident light.</p><p>B. The value of saturation current increases on moving the source of light away from the metal surface.</p><p>C. The maximum kinetic energy of photo-electrons decreases on decreasing the power of LED (light emitting diode) source of light.</p><p>D. The immediate emission of photo-electrons out of metal surface can not be explained by particle nature of light/electromagnetic waves.
E. Existence of threshold wavelength can not be explained by wave nature of light/electromagnetic waves.</p><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** A and B only
- **(B)** A and E only
- **(C)** C and E only
- **(D)** D and E only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) A and E only**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>From Einstein&#39;s photoelectric equation, </p><p>Maximum kinetic energy, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mi>f</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math>, where, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>v</mi><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></msub></math> is maximum velocity of photoelectrons, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi></math> is Planck&#39;s constant, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi></math> is frequency of incident light and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#981;</mi></math> is work function of metal.</p><p>From above relation, we can see that the square of maximum velocity of photoelectrons varies linearly with frequency of incident light.</p><p>If we increase the intensity of the incident radiation by keeping the frequency constant, then the value of saturation current increases in proportion to the intensity of the incident radiation. Thus, saturation current decreases on moving the source of light away from the metal surface.</p></blockquote><blockquote><p>The kinetic energy of the photoelectrons is directly proportional to the light frequency, and it remains constant as light amplitude or power of source increases.</p><p>Photoelectric effect cannot be explained on the basis of wave nature because experimentally a particular energy called the work function of the metal surface in needed to eject electrons from surface, irrespective of the time for which light is incident. </p><p>Photoelectric effect can be explained by particle nature of light (i.e. quantum theory). Threshold wavelength is maximum wavelength at which emission takes place.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q54. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A parallel beam of light of wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>900</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math> and intensity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><msup><mi>Wm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> is incident on a surface perpendicular to the beam. The number of photons crossing <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup></math> area perpendicular to the beam in one second is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>16</mn></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>16</mn></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>17</mn></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>20</mn></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>16</mn></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Wavelength of incident beam <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>900</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p>Energy of a single photon <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac></math>.</p><p>Intensity is defined as power per unit area. Therefore,</p><p>Intensity of incident beam <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>E</mi><mrow><mi>t</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>If the number of photons crossing the given area per second is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math>, then</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>E</mi><mrow><mi>net</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></msub><msub><mi>E</mi><mrow><mi>single</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>pholon</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>I</mi><mi>A</mi><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfenced><mn>100</mn></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mn>900</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>62</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>16</mn></msup></math>.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q55. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>A metal exposed to light of wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>800</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math> and emits photoelectrons with a certain kinetic energy. The maximum kinetic energy of photo-electron doubles when light of wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math> is used. The work function of the metal is</p><p>(Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1230</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>nm</mi></math>).</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>537</mn><mi>eV</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>46</mn><mi>eV</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>615</mn><mi>eV</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>23</mn><mi>eV</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>615</mn><mi>eV</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>According to Einstein&#39;s photoelectric equation</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>k</mi><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1230</mn><mn>800</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>And <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1230</mn><mn>500</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p>Since, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1230</mn><mn>500</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>Eliminating <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> from (1) and (2) we get</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1230</mn><mn>500</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1230</mn><mn>400</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>615</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q56. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>The electric field at a point associated with a light wave is given by
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>200</mn><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>15</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>9</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>15</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Vm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Given: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>14</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>15</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eVs</mi></math></p><p>If this light falls on a metal surface having a work function of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math>, the maximum kinetic energy of the photoelectrons will be</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>90</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>27</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>42</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>42</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>200</mn><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>15</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>9</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>15</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Here, angular velocity, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mn>15</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></msup></math>and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>15</mn></msup></math></p><p>Maximum kinetic energy of the photoelectron is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>KE</mi><mrow><mi>max</mi><mo>.</mo></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mi>f</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>&#969;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>14</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>15</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>15</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>14</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>92</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>42</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q57. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>Given below are two statements: one is labelled as Assertion A and the other is labelled as Reason R.</p><p><strong>Assertion A :</strong> The photoelectric effect does not take place, if the energy of the incident radiation is less than the work function of a metal.</p><p><strong>Reason R :</strong> Kinetic energy of the photoelectrons is zero, if the energy of the incident radiation is equal to the work function of a metal.</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both A and R are correct and R is the correct explanation of A
- **(B)** Both A and R are correct but R is not the correct explanation of A
- **(C)** A is correct but R is not correct
- **(D)** A is not correct but R is correct

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Both A and R are correct but R is not the correct explanation of A**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Work function of a metal is defined as the minimum amount of energy needed for ejection of photoelectron. Therefore, Assertion is correct.</p><p>Incident energy may or may not be completely absorbed by the photoelectron. Therefore, kinetic energy of a photoelectron may be zero even if the incident radiation has energy greater than work function. Hence, though the reason is correct, it is not the correct explanation of Assertion.</p>

</details>

---

### Q58. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> be the maximum kinetic energies of photo-electrons emitted when two monochromatic beams of wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>, respectively are incident on a metallic surface. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> then :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>K</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

According to Einstein&#39;s photoelectric equation

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#981;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mtext>KE</mtext><mtext>max</mtext></msub></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#981;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> is a work function of a metal

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <msub> <mrow> <mi>K</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>h</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> <mi>c</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mi>&#955;</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>-</mo> <msub> <mi>&#981;</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mo>&#8230;</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>i</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

        <math> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>K</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi></mi><mi></mi><mi>h</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> <mi>c</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <msub> <mi>&#955;</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>-</mo> <msub> <mi>&#981;</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mo>&#8230;</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>i</mtext> <mtext>i</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>or</mtext><mspace width="0.5em"/><mspace width="0.5em"/><msub><mi>K</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

                      <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mspace width="0.5em"/><mspace width="0.5em"/><mfenced><mrow><mtext>Given</mtext><mspace width="0.5em"/><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>

                      <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>K</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mi>&#981;</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>Using (ii)</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>or</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>K</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>K</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>K</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msub> <mi>&#981;</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>K</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msub> <mi>&#981;</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>or</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>K</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mo>&#60;</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>K</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q59. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

The stopping potential for photoelectrons emitted from a surface illuminated by light of wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6630</mn><mo>&#8491;</mo></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>42</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>. If the threshold frequency is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>13</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> is (nearest integer): (Given, speed light <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.  Planck&#39;s constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>63</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **35**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>We know that, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mi>f</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p>Or, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>e</mi><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>, where, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><msub><mi>&#957;</mi><mi>th</mi></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> is stopping potential.</p><p>So, threshold frequency is </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#957;</mi><mi>th</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>c</mi><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>e</mi><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mi>h</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>66330</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>19</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>42</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>63</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>630</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>15</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>42</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>63</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>15</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>15</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>630</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>42</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>63</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4524</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1013</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#957;</mi><mi>th</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>35</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>11</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>13</mn></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q60. JEE Main 2022 (24 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The light of two different frequencies whose photons have energies <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>eV</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>eV</mi></math> respectively, illuminate a metallic surface whose work function is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>eV</mi></math> successively. The ratio of maximum speeds of emitted electrons for the two frequencies respectively will be :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>4</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Expression for maximum kinetic energy can be written as, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced><mrow><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced><mrow><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced><mrow><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mfenced><mrow><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mfenced><mrow><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle><mo>&#160;</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></mrow><mrow><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></mrow></mfrac></math> </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p>

</details>
