### Q61. JEE Main 2022 (24 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

When light of frequency twice the threshold frequency is incident on the metal plate, the maximum velocity of emitted electron is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>. When the frequency of incident radiation is increased to five times the threshold value, the maximum velocity of emitted electron becomes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>, the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> will be _____ .

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Maximum kinetic energy is expressed as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mi>f</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math>, here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#981;</mi></math> is work function of metal.</p><p>For first case, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mrow><mi>m</mi><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8722;</mo><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>For second case, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mrow><mi>m</mi><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8722;</mo><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><msup><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q62. JEE Main 2021 (27 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A monochromatic neon lamp with wavelength of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>670</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math> illuminates a photo-sensitive material which has a stopping voltage of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>48</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>. What will be the stopping voltage if the source light is changed with another source of wavelength of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>474</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math> ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>24</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>96</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>eV</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>e</mi><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>V</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>-</mo><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msup><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>'</mo></msup></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msup><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>'</mo></msup></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1240</mn><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>474</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>670</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>48</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mi>eV</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q63. JEE Main 2021 (27 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

In a photoelectric experiment, increasing the intensity of incident light:

**Options:**

- **(A)** increases the number of photons incident and also increases the K.E. of the ejected electrons.
- **(B)** increases the frequency of photons incident and increases the K.E. of the ejected electrons.
- **(C)** increases the number of photons incident and the K.E. of the ejected electrons remains unchanged.
- **(D)** increases the frequency of photons incident and the K.E. of the ejected electrons remains unchanged.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) increases the number of photons incident and the K.E. of the ejected electrons remains unchanged.**

**Detailed Solution:**

The relation between the energy of photons and the intensity of photons is, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>I</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow><mi>n</mi></mfrac></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math> is the number of photons, if we increase the number of photons then frequency does not change it means total energy and kinetic energy of photon remains unchanged but due to to this property the intensity of light is increased.

</details>

---

### Q64. JEE Main 2021 (26 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>In a photoelectric experiment, ultraviolet light of wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>280</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math> is used with lithium cathode having work function <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math>. If the wavelength of incident light is switched to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>400</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math>, find out the change in the stopping potential.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>63</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>From the photoelectric equation,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>e</mi><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>&#8722;</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p>Using this equation for the given two cases,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>e</mi><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>280</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8722;</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>e</mi><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>400</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8722;</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p>On subtracting,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>e</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#8722;</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1240</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mn>120</mn><mrow><mn>280</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>400</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>1.3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#8722;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q65. JEE Main 2021 (27 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An electron and proton are separated by a large distance. The electron starts approaching the proton with energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>eV</mi></math>. The proton captures the electrons and forms a hydrogen atom in second excited state. The resulting photon is incident on a photosensitive metal of threshold wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4000</mn><mi>A</mi></math> What is the maximum kinetic energy of the emitted photoelectron?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>61</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>41</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math>
- **(D)** No photoelectron would be emitted

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>41</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Initially, energy of electron <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>eV</mi></math></p><p>finally, in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">2</mi><mrow><mi>nd</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></msup></math> excited state,</p><p>energy of electron <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>(</mo><mn>13</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>eV</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><msup><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>51</mn><mi>eV</mi></math></p><p>Loss in energy is emitted as photon,</p><p>So, photon energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>51</mn><mi>eV</mi></math></p><p>Now, photoelectric effect equation</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>KE</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>51</mn><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mi>th</mi></msub></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>51</mn><mi>eV</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>12400</mn><mi>eVA</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4000</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>41</mn><mi>eV</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q66. JEE Main 2021 (25 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

A light beam of wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math> is incident on a metal having work function of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math>, placed in a magnetic field of intensity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math>. The electrons emitted perpendicular to the magnetic field <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math>, with maximum kinetic energy are bent into a circular arc of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> is ________<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>.
Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>26</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>, the mass of the electron<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>31</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **125**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>By photoelectric equation</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>max</mi></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1240</mn><mn>500</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#8776;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msqrt><mrow><mi>e</mi><mi>B</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msqrt><mrow><mi>e</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>125</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q67. JEE Main 2021 (25 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

When radiation of wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#955;</mi></math> is incident on a metallic surface, the stopping potential of ejected photoelectrons is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>. If the same surface is illuminated by radiation of double the previous wavelength, then the stopping potential becomes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>. The threshold wavelength of the metal is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">S</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mi>v</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>ii</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>Using above equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>)</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>ii</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>iii</mi><mo>)</mo></math> </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Put in equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>ii</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mi>th</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mi>th</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q68. JEE Main 2021 (20 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

A certain metallic surface is illuminated by monochromatic radiation of wavelength $\lambda$. The stopping potential for photoelectric current for this radiation is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msub><mrow><mo mathvariant="italic">&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="italic">V</mi></mrow><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>.</mo></math> If the same surface is illuminated with a radiation of wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>,</mo></math> the stopping potential is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>.</mo></math> The threshold wavelength of this surface for photoelectric effect is___________<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>.</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KE</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>e</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>e</mi><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced></math></p><p>Using <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced><mtext>&#160;and</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced></math>,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mi>t</mi></msub></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mi>t</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p>

</details>

### Q69. JEE Main 2021 (17 Mar Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Two identical photocathodes receive the light of frequencies <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>f</mi><mn mathvariant="italic">2</mn></msub></math> respectively. If the velocities of the photo-electrons coming out are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>v</mi><mn mathvariant="italic">1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> respectively, then

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn mathvariant="italic">1</mn><mn mathvariant="italic">2</mn></msubsup><mo mathvariant="italic">-</mo><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn mathvariant="italic">2</mn><mn mathvariant="italic">2</mn></msubsup><mo mathvariant="italic">=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn mathvariant="italic">2</mn><mo mathvariant="italic">&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="italic">h</mi></mrow><mrow><mo mathvariant="italic">&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="italic">m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mstyle mathvariant="italic"><mrow><mo>[</mo><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>]</mo></mrow></mstyle></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>h</mi></mrow><mi>m</mi></mfrac><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mo>[</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>]</mo></mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn mathvariant="italic">1</mn><mn mathvariant="italic">2</mn></msubsup><mo mathvariant="italic">-</mo><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn mathvariant="italic">2</mn><mn mathvariant="italic">2</mn></msubsup><mo mathvariant="italic">=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn mathvariant="italic">2</mn><mo mathvariant="italic">&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="italic">h</mi></mrow><mrow><mo mathvariant="italic">&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="italic">m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mstyle mathvariant="italic"><mrow><mo>[</mo><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>]</mo></mrow></mstyle></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>-</mo><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>f</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></p>

</details>

### Q70. JEE Main 2021 (16 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The stopping potential in the context of photoelectric effect depends on the following property of incident electromagnetic radiation:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Phase
- **(B)** Intensity
- **(C)** Amplitude
- **(D)** Frequency

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Frequency**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>According to Einstein&rsquo;s photoelectric equation, stopping potential depends on frequency as</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi><mi>&#957;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>h</mi><msub><mi>&#957;</mi><mo>&#8728;</mo></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>e</mi><mi>V</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>&#957;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>h</mi><msub><mi>&#957;</mi><mo>&#8728;</mo></msub></mrow><mi>e</mi></mfrac></math></p><p>Hence stopping potential depends on frequency.</p>

</details>

### Q71. JEE Main 2021 (26 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

Two stream of photons, possessing energies equal to twice and ten times the work function of metal are incident on the metal surface successively. The value of ratio of maximum velocities of the photoelectrons emitted in the two respective cases is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>3</mn></math> The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> is

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mi>&#957;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mi>&#957;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>&#957;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mi>m</mi></mfrac></msqrt></math></p><p>Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi><msub><mi>&#957;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi><msub><mi>&#957;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><msub><mi>&#957;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>h</mi><msub><mi>&#957;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>10</mn><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q72. JEE Main 2021 (25 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The stopping potential for electrons emitted from a photosensitive surface illuminated by light of wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>491</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>710</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>.</mo></math> When the incident wavelength is changed to a new value, the stopping potential is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>43</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>.</mo></math> The new wavelength is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>382</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>309</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>329</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>400</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>382</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>e</mi><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>s</mi></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1240</mn><mn>491</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>71</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1240</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>43</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>382</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>nm</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q73. JEE Main 2021 (24 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>Given below are two statements:</p><p><strong>Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>:</mo></math></strong> Two photons having equal linear momenta have equal wavelengths.</p><p><strong>Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>II</mi><mo>:</mo></math></strong> If the wavelength of the photon is decreased, then the momentum and energy of a photon will also decrease.</p><p>In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below.</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math> and Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>II</mi></math> are false
- **(B)** Both Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math> and Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>II</mi></math> are true
- **(C)** Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math> is true but Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>II</mi></math> is false
- **(D)** Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math> is false but Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>II</mi></math> is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math> is true but Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>II</mi></math> is false**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>If linear momentum are equal then wavelength also equal.</p><p>The momentum <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi></math> and energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi></math> of a photon is given by,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>h</mi><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac></math>; where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi></math> is speed of light and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi></math> is Plank&#39;s constant.</p><p>On decreasing wavelength, momentum and energy of photon increases.</p>

</details>

### Q74. JEE Main 2020 (05 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

The surface of a metal is illuminated alternately with photons of energies <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math> respectively. The ratio of maximum speeds of the photoelectrons emitted in the two cases is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo></math> The work function of the metal in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>eV</mi><mo>)</mo></math> is..........

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given,</p><p>Energy of two photons are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math></p><p>The ratio of maximum speeds of the photoelectrons emitted in the two cases is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p>Using Einstein equation of photoelectric effect,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>Where, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#981;</mi></math> is the work function of metal and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi></math> is the energy of photon</p><p>Now using equation for both the cases we get,</p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>Dividing equation (2) and (3) and substitute given values, we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q75. JEE Main 2020 (04 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>In a photoelectric effect experiment, the graph of stopping potential <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi></math> versus reciprocal of wavelength obtained is shown in the figure. As the intensity of incident radiation is increased : </p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/58673523-de47-4939-936d-71af542b197f-image.png" style="width: 246px; height: 230px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Straight line shifts to right
- **(B)** Slope of the straight line get more steep
- **(C)** Straight line shifts to left
- **(D)** Graph does not change

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Graph does not change**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Einstein&#39;s photoelectric equation is given by,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>eV</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mi>&#957;</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>o</mi></msub></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>o</mi></msub></math> is the work function.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>e&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>o</mi></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mfrac></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#957;</mi><mi>&#955;</mi></math>.</p><p>Intensity does not affect the frequency and work function of a material. Therefore, the graph does not change with increasing intensity.</p>

</details>

### Q76. JEE Main 2020 (04 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>Given figure shows few data points in a photo-electric effect experiment for a certain metal. The minimum energy for ejection of electrons from its surface is: (Planck&#39;s constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6.62</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup><mtext>&#160;J.s</mtext></math>)</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/815b342a-4bbb-4c59-a90e-67871aeaa2ec-image.png" style="width: 173px; height: 181px;" /> </p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mrow><mn>2.27</mn><mtext>&#160;eV</mtext></mrow></math>
- **(B)** <math><mrow><mn>2.59</mn><mtext>&#160;eV</mtext></mrow></math>
- **(C)** <math><mrow><mn>1.93</mn><mtext>&#160;eV</mtext></mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math><mrow><mn>2.10</mn><mtext>&#160;eV</mtext></mrow></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mrow><mn>2.27</mn><mtext>&#160;eV</mtext></mrow></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4611faca-a18b-47f3-afe6-6284be1dc606-image.png" style="width: 300px; height: 249px;" /></p><p>Threshold Energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>hv</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>.62</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.5</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>14</mn></msup><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p>Work friction <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>.62</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.5</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>20</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.6</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>19</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mi>eV</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.27</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi>eV</mi></math> </p>

</details>

### Q77. JEE Main 2020 (03 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

When the wavelength of radiation falling on a metal is changed from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>500</mn><mi>nm</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>to</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>200</mn><mi>nm</mi></math>, the maximum kinetic energy of the photoelectrons becomes three times larger. The work function of the metal is close to:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>81</mn><mi>eV</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn><mi>eV</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>52</mn><mi>eV</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>61</mn><mi>eV</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>61</mn><mi>eV</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>KE</mi><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><mn>500</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#981;</mi></math>   .....(i)</p><p>Now,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>KE</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><mn>200</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#981;</mi></math></p><p>   .....(ii)</p><p>From equation (i) and (ii)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><mn>200</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#981;</mi></mstyle><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><mn>500</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#981;</mi></mstyle></mfrac></math></p><p>Put the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>hc</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1237</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math> and solving <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>61</mn><mi>eV</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q78. JEE Main 2020 (02 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

When radiation of wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is used to illuminate a metallic surface, the stopping potential is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>. When the same surface is illuminated with radiation of wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>, the stopping potential is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>. If the threshold wavelength for the metallic surface is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n&#955;</mi></math> then value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></math> will be :

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **9**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>e</mi><mi>V</mi></math> ...........(1)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>e</mi><mi>V</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>      ---------(2)</p><p>from (1) &amp; (2)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mi>e</mi><mi>v</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mi>eV</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>eV</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>8</mn><mn>9</mn></mfrac><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>8</mn><mn>9</mn></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><mrow><mn>9</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mi>th</mi></msub></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mrow><mi>t</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q79. JEE Main 2020 (07 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

A beam of electromagnetic radiation of intensity <math> <mrow> <mn>6.4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mrow><mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mn>10</mn></mrow> <mrow> <mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow> </msup> <mtext>W</mtext></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mtext>cm</mtext></mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow></mrow> </mrow> </math> is comprised of wavelength, <math><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>310</mn><mi></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> . It falls normally on a metal (work function <math><mi>&#966;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>e</mi><mi>V</mi></math> ) of surface area of <math><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> . If one in <math><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math> photons ejects an election, total number of electrons ejected in <math><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math> is <math><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></msup></math> . <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1240</mn><mi></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1.6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>19</mn></mrow></msup><mi>J</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mi></mi></math> then <math><mi>x</mi></math> is ___________

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **11**

**Detailed Solution:**

Energy of photon. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1240</mn><mrow><mn>310</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mtext>nm</mtext></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> (So photoelectric effect will take place)
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1.6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>19</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>6.4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>19</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></math>
Number of photons falling per second
<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>6.4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>6.4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>19</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>14</mn></mrow></msup></math>
Number of photoelectron emitted per second
<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>14</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>11</mn></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

---

### Q80. JEE Main 2019 (12 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The stopping potential <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msub></math> (in volt) as a function of frequency <math><mo>(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>)</mo></math> for a sodium emitter, is shown in the figure. The work function of sodium, form the data plotted in the figure, will be:
(Given: Planck&rsquo;s constant <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>6.63</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math> , electron charge <math> <mrow> <mrow><mo>(</mo> <mtext>e</mtext> <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow> </math> <math><mo>=</mo><mn>1.6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>19</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ebbd2ff1-374e-4682-aec4-feedb380c9db-image2.png" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>2.12</mn><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>1.82</mn><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>1.95</mn><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>1.66</mn><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mn>1.66</mn><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>From Einstein&#39;s photoelectric equation, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi><mi>&#957;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>e</mi><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo></math>.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>&#957;</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>&#981;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#957;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>14</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Hz</mi></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>.</p><p>Putting the value in equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math>, we get</p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>hv</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>&#981;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mfrac></math></p><p>So, Work function <math><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#981;</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#957;</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow></mfrac><mtext>&#8201;&#8201;</mtext><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>6.63</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>14</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>1.6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>19</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1.66</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q81. JEE Main 2019 (10 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A <math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></math> laser operates at a wavelength of <math><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>500</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> . The number of photons that will be emitted per second is:
[Given Planck&#39;s constant <math><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6.6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>,</mo></math> speed of light <math><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3.0</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mrow></math> ]

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>1.5</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>16</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>5</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>15</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>16</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>1</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>16</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mn>5</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>15</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></math>  
<math><mi>λ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>500</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>  
Number of photons will be emitted per second is, 
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>×</mo><mn>500</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>6.6</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup><mo>×</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>  
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>15</mn></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

### Q82. JEE Main 2019 (10 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Light is incident normally on a completely absorbing surface with an energy flux of <math><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>25</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> . If the surface has an area of <math><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>25</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> , the momentum transferred to the surface in <math> <mrow> <mtext>40</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>min</mtext></mrow> </math> time duration will be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>6.3</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>5.0</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>3.5</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>1.4</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mn>5.0</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The power of the incident light is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn><mtext>&#8201;&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>25</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>625</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></math>
Energy incident per second, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>625</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
Momentum incident per second, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>E</mi><mi>c</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>625</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mfrac></math>
<math><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> Total momentum transferred to the surface <math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>625</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>60</mn></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>

</details>

### Q83. JEE Main 2019 (10 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

In a photoelectric effect experiment, the threshold wavelength of light is<math><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>380</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> . If the wavelength of incident light is<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>260</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> , the maximum kinetic energy of emitted electrons will be
Given <math><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></math> (in <math><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> ) <math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1237</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#955;</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfrac></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>4.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>3.0</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>1.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>15.1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mn>1.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

From Einstein’s photoelectric equation, 
<math><mi>K</mi><mo>.</mo><msub><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>λ</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math> (here, <math><msub><mrow><mi>λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo></math> threshold wavelength) 
<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1237</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>260</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1237</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>380</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>  
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>1237</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>380</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>260</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>380</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>260</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>  
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>1.5</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

</details>

### Q84. JEE Main 2019 (09 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The electric field of light wave is given as <math><mi>&#160;</mi><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>14</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow><mover><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>^</mo></mrow></mover><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow></mfrac></math> . This light falls on a metal plate of work function <math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> . The stopping potential of the photo-electrons is:
Given, <math><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></math> (in <math><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> ) <math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>12375</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#955;</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#197;</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfrac></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>0.72</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>2.0</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>2.48</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>0.48</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mn>0.48</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mo>×</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>14</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mtext> </mtext><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow></mrow></math>  
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>  
And <math><mi mathvariant="normal">ω</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>14</mn></mrow></msup><mo>×</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi></math> <math><mo>⇒</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>14</mn></mrow></msup><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></math>  
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">ϕ</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></msub></math>  
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">ϕ</mi></math>  
<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>6.6</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup><mo>×</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>14</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>1.6</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>19</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></math>  
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>2.475</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0.48</mn><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>  
<math><mo>∴</mo></math> Stopping potential is <math><mn>0.48</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math>

</details>

### Q85. JEE Main 2019 (12 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

When a certain photosensitive surface is illuminated with a monochromatic light of frequency <math><mi>&#957;</mi><mo>,</mo></math> the stopping potential of the photo current is  <math><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>.</mo></math>  When the surface is illuminated by monochromatic light of frequency <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#957;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo></math> the stopping potential is <math><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>.</mo></math> The threshold frequency for photoelectric emission is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>v</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>2</mn><mi>v</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>h</mi><mi>ν​</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>e</mi></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>ν​</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>e</mi></math>

On solving, we get, <math><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>h</mi><mi> </mi><mi>ν​</mi></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi>h</mi><msub><mrow><mi>ν​</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>h</mi><mi> </mi><mi>ν​</mi></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><msub><mrow><mi>ν​</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>ν​</mi></math>

</details>

### Q86. JEE Main 2019 (11 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In a photoelectric experiment, the wavelength of the light incident on a metal is changed from $300 \mathrm{nm}$ to $400 \mathrm{nm}$. The decrease in the stopping potential is close to :

$\left(\frac{\mathrm{hc}}{\mathrm{e}}=1240 \mathrm{nm}-\mathrm{V}\right)$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.5 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $1.5 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $1.0 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $2.0 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $1.0 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\phi=$ work function of the metal,

$\frac{\mathrm{hc}}{\lambda_{1}}=\phi+\mathrm{eV}_{1}$

$\frac{\mathrm{hc}}{\lambda_{2}}=\phi+\mathrm{eV}_{2} \quad \ldots \ldots$

(ii)

Sutracting (ii) from (i) we get

$\mathrm{hc}\left(\frac{1}{\lambda_{1}}-\frac{1}{\lambda_{2}}\right)=\mathrm{e}\left(\mathrm{V}_{1}-\mathrm{V}_{2}\right)$

$\Rightarrow V_{1}-V_{2}=\frac{h c}{e}\left(\frac{\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}}{\lambda_{1} \cdot \lambda_{2}}\right)\left[\begin{array}{l}\lambda_{1}=300 \mathrm{nm} \\ \lambda_{2}=400 \mathrm{nm} \\ \frac{\mathrm{hc}}{\mathrm{e}}=1240 \mathrm{nm}-\mathrm{V}\end{array}\right]$

$=(1240 \mathrm{nm}-\mathrm{v})\left(\frac{100 \mathrm{nm}}{300 \mathrm{nm} \times 400 \mathrm{nm}}\right)$

$=1,03 \mathrm{~V} \approx 1 \mathrm{~V}$

</details>

### Q87. JEE Main 2019 (10 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A metal plate of area <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> is illuminated by a radiation of intensity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>16</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>i</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>W</mi></mrow><msup><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math> . The work function of the metal is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>e</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>.</mo></math> The energy of the incident photons is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>e</mi><mi>V</mi></math> and only <math><mn>10</mn><mi>%</mi></math> of it produces photo electrons. The number of emitted photo electron  per second and their maximum energy, respectively, will be: <math><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>e</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1.6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>19</mn></mrow></msup><mi>J</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>14</mn></mrow></msup></math>  and  <math><mn>10</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>e</mi><mi>V</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>12</mn></mrow></msup></math>  and  <math><mn>5</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>e</mi><mi>V</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>11</mn></mrow></msup></math>  and  <math><mn>5</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>e</mi><mi>V</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow></msup></math> and  <math><mn>5</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>e</mi><mi>V</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>11</mn></mrow></msup></math>  and  <math><mn>5</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>e</mi><mi>V</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Einstein&#39;s photoelectric equation
Maximum <math><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>e</mi><mi>V</mi></math>

Intensity <math><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow><mrow><mi>t</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>I</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>

and number of electrons per second. <math><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>%</mi></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>I</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>16</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1.6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>19</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>11</mn></mrow></msup></math> per sec

</details>
