### Q88. JEE Main 2019 (09 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The magnetic field associated with a light wave is given, at the origin, by <math><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi> </mi><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3.14</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mi>c</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>6.28</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mi>c</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfenced><mo>.</mo></math> If this light falls on a silver plate having a work function of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.7</mo><mi>eV</mi><mo>,</mo></math> what will be the maximum kinetic energy of the photoelectrons?

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mtext> </mtext><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>.6</mo><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>.82</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>eV</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn><mo>.72</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi>eV</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><mo>.5</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>eV</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>.52</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>eV</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn><mo>.72</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi>eV</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>0</mi></mrow></msub><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3.14</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mi>c</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>6.28</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mi>c</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfenced></math>

Maximum <math><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi></math> will associate with the maximum frequency

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>f</mi><mrow><mi>max</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>6.28</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>7</mn></msup><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>7</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Hz</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>15</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>Hz</mi></math>

<math><mi>K</mi><msub><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><msub><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>.63</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>15</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.6</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>19</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.7</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>eV</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>.72</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>eV</mi></math>

</details>

### Q89. JEE Main 2019 (09 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The surface of certain metal is first illuminated with light of wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>λ</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>350</mn><mi> </mi><mn>n</mn><mn>m</mn></math> and then, by a light of wavelength <math><msub><mrow><mi>λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>540</mn><mi> </mi><mn>n</mn><mn>m</mn><mo>.</mo></math> It is found that the maximum speed of the photoelectrons in the two cases differ by a factor of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>. The work function of the metal (in <math><mn>e</mn><mn>V</mn></math>) is close to

(Energy of photon <math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1240</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>λ</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mi> </mi><mn>e</mn><mn>V</mn></math> )

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>2.5</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>1.8</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>5.6</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>1.4</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mn>1.8</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>350</mn><mn>&#160;n</mn><mn>m</mn><mo>,</mo><mtext>&#8201;&#8201;</mtext><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>540</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mn>n</mn><mn>m</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mtext>&#160;</mtext><mfrac><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mtext>V</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mtext>V</mtext><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msub><mtext>V</mtext><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>V</mi></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>V</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#981;</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>540</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>340</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#981;</mi></math>

<math><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1.8</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>e</mn><mn>V</mn></math>

</details>

---

### Q90. JEE Main 2017 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A laser light of wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>660</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8201;</mi><mi>nm</mi></math> is used to weld Retina detachment. If a laser pulse of width <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8201;</mi><mi>ms</mi></math> and power <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">0</mi><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kW</mi></math> is used, the approximate number of photons in the pulse are (Take Planck&#39;s Constant, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6.62</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8201;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>22</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>19</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>20</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>18</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>20</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The energy of photon is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac></math>.</p><p>Power<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>P</mi></mfenced></math> is given as, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>E</mi><mi>t</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>From the above equation, the number of photons is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>&#955;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mn>0.5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>660</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>6.6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>34</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>

Hence, number of photons in the pulse are<math><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>18</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>20</mn></mrow></msup></math>.</p>

</details>

---

### Q91. JEE Main 2017 (08 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The maximum velocity of the photoelectrons emitted from the surface is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi></math> when light of frequency <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math> falls on a metal surface. If the incident frequency is increased to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>n</mi></math>, the maximum velocity of the ejected photoelectrons will be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** more than <math><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi></mi><mi>v</mi></math>
- **(B)** equal to <math><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi>v</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi>v</mi></math>
- **(D)** less than  <math><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi>v</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) more than <math><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi></mi><mi>v</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>According to Einstein&#39;s photoelectric effect, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math></p><p>The incident energy of the photon is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mi>n</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math>

If incident frequency increased to 3n

<math><msub><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>h</mi><mi>n</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#981;</mi></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>n</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#981;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><msub><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#981;</mi></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#981;</mi></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#981;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>v</mi><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q92. JEE Main 2016 (10 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A photoelectric surface is illuminated successively by monochromatic light of wavelengths <math><mi>&#955;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>d&#160;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>.</mo></math> If the maximum kinetic energy of the emitted photoelectrons in the second case is 3 times that in the first case, the work function of the surface is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>λ</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>λ</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>λ</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>λ</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>λ</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>K</mi><mo>.</mo><msub><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>λ</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>λ</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>ϕ</mi></math>

<math><mi>K</mi><mo>.</mo><msub><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>ϕ</mi></math>

<math><mi>K</mi><mo>.</mo><msub><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>K</mi><mo>.</mo><msub><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>λ</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>ϕ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>c</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>λ</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

---

### Q93. JEE Main 2016 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

When photons of wavelength <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#955;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></math> are incident on an isolated sphere, the corresponding stopping potential is found to be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi></math>. When photons of wavelength <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#955;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> are used, the corresponding stopping potential was thrice that of the above value. If light of wavelength <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#955;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></math> is used then find the stopping potential for this case:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> ...(i)

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">3</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></math> ...(ii)

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">′</mi></mrow></msup></math> ....(iii)

Equation (i) and (ii) 

<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">′</mi></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">′</mi></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

### Q94. JEE Main 2016 (03 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Radiation of wavelength <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></math> is incident on a photocell. The fastest emitted photoelectron has a speed <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></math>. If the wavelength is changed to <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">λ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>, the speed of the fastest emitted photoelectron will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mi>mv</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#981;</mi></math> ...(i)

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>mv</mi><msup><mo>&#39;</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#981;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></math> ...(ii)

From Eqn. (i)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#955;</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mi>mv</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#981;</mi></math>

On putting this Equation (ii)

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mi>mv</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8242;</mi><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mi>mv</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#981;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#981;</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><msup><mo>&#39;</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#981;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>&#39;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q95. JEE Main 2014 (12 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A beam of light has two wavelengths of $4972 Å$ and $6216 Å$ with a total intensity of $3.6 \times 10^{-3}$ $\mathrm{Wm}^{-2}$ equally distributed among the two wavelengths. The beam falls normally on an area of $1 \mathrm{~cm}^2$ of a clean metallic surface of work function $2.3 \mathrm{eV}$. Assume that there is no loss of light by reflection and that each capable photon ejects one electron. The number of photoelectrons liberated in $2 \mathrm{~s}$ is approximately:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $6 \times 10^{11}$
- **(B)** $9 \times 10^{11}$
- **(C)** $11 \times 10^{11}$
- **(D)** $15 \times 10^{11}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $9 \times 10^{11}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given, $\lambda_1=4972 Å$
and $\lambda_2=6216 Å$
and $\mathrm{I}=3.6 \times 10^{-3} \mathrm{Wm}^{-2}$
Intensity associated with each wavelength
$$
=\frac{3.6 \times 10^{-3}}{2}=1.8 \times 10^{-3} \mathrm{Wm}^{-2}
$$
Work function $\phi=\mathrm{hv}=\frac{\mathrm{hc}}{\lambda}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
&=\frac{\left(6.62 \times 10^{-34}\right)\left(3 \times 10^8\right)}{\lambda} \\
&=\frac{12.4 \times 10^3}{\lambda} \mathrm{ev}
\end{aligned}
$$


for different wavelengths
$$
\begin{array}{r}
\phi_1=\frac{12.4 \times 10^3}{\lambda_1}=\frac{12.4 \times 10^3}{4972}=2.493 \mathrm{eV} \\
=3.984 \times 10^{-19} \mathrm{~J} \\
\phi_2=\frac{12.4 \times 10^3}{\lambda_2}=\frac{12.4 \times 10^3}{6216}=1.994 \mathrm{eV} \\
=3.184 \times 10^{-19} \mathrm{~J}
\end{array}
$$
Work function for metallic surface $\phi=2.3$ $\mathrm{eV}$ (given)
$\phi_2 < \phi$
Therefore, $\phi_2$ will not contribute in this process.
Now, no. of electrons per $\mathrm{m}^2-\mathrm{s}=$ no. of photons per $\mathrm{m}^2-\mathrm{s}$
no. of electrons per $\mathrm{m}^2-\mathrm{s}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
&=\frac{1.8 \times 10^{-3}}{3.984 \times 10^{-19}} \times 10^{-4} \\
&\left(\because 1 \mathrm{~cm}^2=10^{-4} \mathrm{~m}^2\right)=0.45 \times 10^{12}
\end{aligned}
$$
So, the number of photo electrons liberated
in 2 sec.
$=0.45 \times 10^{12} \times 2$
$=9 \times 10^{11}$

</details>

### Q97. JEE Main 2013 (25 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

In an experiment on photoelectric effect, a student plots stopping potential $\mathrm{V}_0$ against reciprocal of the wavelength $\lambda$ of the incident light for two different metals $\mathrm{A}$ and $\mathrm{B}$. These are shown in the figure.
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/faOYmrbqWRcGJoMuCgItvLCHwkGf_dmQqrauDE7QQHc.original.fullsize.png">

Looking at the graphs, you can most appropriately say that:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Work function of metal $\mathrm{B}$ is greater than that of metal $\mathrm{A}$
- **(B)** For light of certain wavelength falling on both metal, maximum kinetic energy of electrons emitted from A will be greater than those emitted from $\mathrm{B}$.
- **(C)** Work function of metal $\mathrm{A}$ is greater than that of metal $\mathrm{B}$
- **(D)** Students data is not correct

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Students data is not correct**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned}
& \frac{\mathrm{hc}}{\lambda}-\phi=\mathrm{eV}_0 \\
& \mathrm{v}_0=\frac{\mathrm{hc}}{\mathrm{e} \lambda}-\frac{\phi}{\mathrm{e}}
\end{aligned}$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/cPf614jUcHMLzQLkKLWRalFj_oMNfjqTy6dy_pI8pXg.original.fullsize.png">

As the value of $\frac{1}{\lambda}$ (increasing and decreasing) is not specified hence we cannot say that which metal has comparatively greater or lesser work function $(\phi)$.

</details>

The anode voltage of a photocell is kept fixed. The wavelength <math> <mrow> <mi>&#955;</mi> </mrow> </math> of the light falling on the cathode is gradually changed. The plate current <math> <mrow> <mtext>I</mtext> </mrow> </math> of the photocell varies as follows :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bac9364e-8194-48df-babf-23c2497da2c0-image.png" style="width: 206px; height: 176px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/88459f24-1f1d-4954-9655-bece52b4592c-image.png" style="width: 206px; height: 201px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/638021fa-6e3a-4c5e-96be-61de227f1917-image.png" style="width: 214px; height: 210px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9b23dfa0-28a0-459c-a72e-2fc0dd98a020-image.png" style="width: 206px; height: 199px;" />

### Q99. JEE Main 2012 (26 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Photoelectrons are ejected from a metal when light of frequency $v$ falls on it. Pick out the wrong statement from the following.

**Options:**

- **(A)** No electrons are emitted if $v$ is less than $W / h$, where $W$ is the work function of the metal
- **(B)** The ejection of the photoelectrons is instantaneous.
- **(C)** The maximum energy of the photoelectrons is hu.
- **(D)** The maximum energy of the photoelectrons is independent of the intensity of the light.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) The maximum energy of the photoelectrons is hu.**

**Detailed Solution:**

According to photo-electric equation : $\mathrm{K} . \mathrm{E}_{\max }=\mathrm{h} v-\mathrm{h} v_0$ (Work function) Some sort of energy is used in ejecting the photoelectrons.

</details>

### Q100. JEE Main 2012 (19 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

This question has Statement 1 and Statement 2. Of the four choices given after the Statements, choose the one that best describes the two Statements.
Statement 1: A metallic surface is irradiated by a monochromatic light of frequency $v>v_0$ (the threshold frequency). If the incident frequency is now doubled, the photocurrent and the maximum kinetic energy are also doubled.
Statement 2: The maximum kinetic energy of photoelectrons emitted from a surface is linearly dependent on the frequency of the incident light. The photocurrent depends only on the intensity of the incident light.

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement 1 is true, Statement 2 is true, Statement 2 is the correct explanation of Statement 1.
- **(B)** Statement 1 is false, Statement 2 is true.
- **(C)** Statement 1 is true, Statement 2 is false.
- **(D)** Statement 1 is true, Statement 2 is true, Statement 2 is not the correct explanation of Statement 1.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Statement 1 is false, Statement 2 is true.**

**Detailed Solution:**

The maximum kinetic energy of photoelectrons depends upon frequency on incident light and photo current depends upon intensity of incident light.

</details>

### Q101. JEE Main 2011

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

This question has Statement $-1$ and Statement $-2$. Of the four choices given after the statements, choose the one that best describes the two statements.
Statement-1 : A metallic surface is irradiated by a monochromatic light of frequency $v>v_0$ (the threshold frequency). The maximum kinetic energy and the stopping potential are $\mathrm{K}_{\max }$ and $\mathrm{V}_0$ respectively. If the frequency incident on the surface doubled, both the $\mathrm{K}_{\max }$ and $\mathrm{V}_0$ are also doubled.
Statement-2 : The maximum kinetic energy and the stopping potential of photoelectrons emitted from a surface are linearly dependent on the frequency of incident light.

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement-1 is true, Statement-2 is true; Statement-2 is the correct explanation of Statement-1.
- **(B)** Statement-1 is true, Statement-2 is true; Statement-2 is not the correct explanation of Statement-1.
- **(C)** Statement-1 is false, Statement-2 is true.
- **(D)** Statement-1 is true, Statement-2 is false.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Statement-1 is false, Statement-2 is true.**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{KE}_{\max }=\mathrm{hu}-\mathrm{hu}_0 \\
& \mathrm{hu}-\mathrm{hu}=\mathrm{e} \times \Delta \mathrm{v} \\
& \mathrm{V}_0=\frac{\mathrm{hu}}{\mathrm{e}}-\frac{\mathrm{hv_{0 }}}{\mathrm{e}}
\end{aligned}
$$
' $v$ ' is doubled
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{KE}_{\max }=2 \mathrm{hu}-\mathrm{hu}_0 \\
& \mathrm{~V}_0^{\prime}=(\Delta \mathrm{V})^{\prime}=\frac{2 \mathrm{hu}}{\mathrm{e}}-\frac{h \mathrm{u}_0}{\mathrm{e}}
\end{aligned}
$$
$\frac{\mathrm{KE}_{\max }}{\mathrm{KE}_{\max }}$ may not be equal to 2
$\Rightarrow \frac{\mathrm{V}_0^{\prime}}{\mathrm{V}_0}$ may not equal to 2
$\mathrm{KE} \max =\mathrm{hu}-\mathrm{hv}_0$
$$
\mathrm{V}=\frac{h \mathrm{v}}{\mathrm{e}}-\frac{h \mathrm{~h}_0}{\mathrm{e}}
$$

</details>

### Q102. JEE Main 2010

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Statement-1 : When ultraviolet light is incident on a photocell, its stopping potential is $V_0$ and the maximum kinetic energy of the photoelectrons is $\mathrm{K}_{\max }$. When the ultraviolet light is replaced by $\mathrm{X}-$ rays, both $\mathrm{V}_0$ and $\mathrm{K}_{\max }$ increase.
Statement-2 : Photoelectrons are emitted with speeds ranging from zero to a maximum value because of the range of frequencies present in the incident light. 
Of the four choices given after the statements, choose the one that best describes the two statements.

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement-1 is true, Statement-2 is true; Statement-2 is the correct explanation of Statement-1.
- **(B)** Statement-1 is true, Statement-2 is true; Statement-2 is not the correct explanation of Statement-
1.
- **(C)** Statement-1 is false, Statement-2 is true.
- **(D)** Statement-1 is true, Statement- 2 is false.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Statement-1 is true, Statement- 2 is false.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Since the frequency of ultraviolet light is less than the frequency of $X$-rays, the energy of each incident photon will be more for X-rays
$K.E_{photoelectron} =h v-\varphi$
Stopping potential is to stop the fastest photoelectron
$$
\mathrm{V}_0=
$$
so, $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{E}} \mathrm{E}_{\max }$ and $\mathrm{V}_0$ both increases.
But K.E ranges from zero to K.E $E_{\max }$ because of loss of energy due to subsequent collisions before getting ejected and not due to range of frequencies in the incident light.

</details>

### Q103. JEE Main 2009

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The surface of a metal is illuminated with the light of $400 \mathrm{~nm}$. The kinetic energy of the ejected photoelectrons was found to be $1.68 \mathrm{eV}$. The work function of the metal is $(\mathrm{hc}=1240 \mathrm{eV} \mathrm{nm})$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3.09 \mathrm{eV}$
- **(B)** $1.41 \mathrm{eV}$
- **(C)** $151 \mathrm{eV}$
- **(D)** $1.68 \mathrm{eV}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $1.41 \mathrm{eV}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& \frac{1}{2} \mathrm{mv}^2=\mathrm{eV}_{\mathrm{o}}=1.68 \mathrm{eV} \Rightarrow \mathrm{hv}=\frac{\mathrm{hc}}{\lambda}=\frac{1240 \mathrm{evnm}}{400 \mathrm{~nm}}=3.1 \mathrm{eV} \Rightarrow 3.1 \mathrm{eV}=\mathrm{W}_0+1.6 \mathrm{eV} \\
& \therefore \quad \mathrm{W}_0=1.42 \mathrm{eV}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

The threshold frequency for a metallic surface corresponds to an energy of $6.2 \mathrm{eV}$, and the stopping potential for a radiation incident on this surface $5 \mathrm{~V}$. The incident radiation lies in

**Options:**

- **(A)** X-ray region
- **(B)** ultra-violet region

- **(D)** visible region

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

### Q106. JEE Main 2005

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A photocell is illuminated by a small bright source placed $1 \mathrm{~m}$ away. When the same source of light is placed $\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{~m}$ away, the number of electrons emitted by photo cathode would

**Options:**

- **(A)** decrease by a factor of 4
- **(B)** increase by a factor of 4
- **(C)** decrease by a factor of 2
- **(D)** increase by a factor of 2

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) increase by a factor of 4**

**Detailed Solution:**

$I \propto \frac{1}{r^2}$

</details>

### Q107. JEE Main 2004

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The work function of a substance is $4.0 \mathrm{eV}$. Then longest wavelength of light that can cause photoelectron emission from this substance approximately

**Options:**

- **(A)** $540 \mathrm{~nm}$
- **(B)** $400 \mathrm{~nm}$
- **(C)** $310 \mathrm{~nm}$
- **(D)** $220 \mathrm{~nm}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $310 \mathrm{~nm}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\frac{\mathrm{hc}}{\lambda}=\mathrm{W}$
$\lambda_{\text {longest }}=\frac{\mathrm{hc}}{\mathrm{W}}=\frac{6.6 \times 10^{-34} \times 3 \times 10^8}{4.0 \times 1.6 \times 10^{-19}}$
$\Rightarrow \lambda_{\text {longest }} \approx 310 \mathrm{~nm}$.

</details>

### Q109. JEE Main 2004

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A radiation of energy $E$ falls normally on a perfectly reflecting surface. The momentum transferred to the surface is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $E / c$
- **(B)** $2 \mathrm{E} / \mathrm{c}$
- **(C)** Ec
- **(D)** $\mathrm{E} / \mathrm{c}^2$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $2 \mathrm{E} / \mathrm{c}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\Delta \mathrm{P}_{\text {surface }}=-\Delta \mathrm{P}=\frac{2 \mathrm{E}}{\mathrm{C}}$.

</details>

### Q111. JEE Main 2002

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Sodium and copper have work functions $2.3 \mathrm{eV}$ and $4.5 \mathrm{eV}$ respectively. Then the ratio of the wave lengths is nearest to

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1 : 2
- **(B)** 4 : 1
- **(C)** 2 : 1
- **(D)** 1 : 4

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 2 : 1**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{hc} / \lambda_0=\mathrm{W}_0 ; \frac{\left(\lambda_0\right)_1}{\left(\lambda_0\right)_2}=\frac{\left(\mathrm{W}_0\right)_2}{\left(\mathrm{~W}_0\right)_1}=\frac{4.5}{2.3}=2: 1$

</details>
